# test(gemm): cover public INT8 large-accumulator contracts

[Upstream PR](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1265) · state: closed · merged: True

## Original PR description

## Summary

Add exact INT8→INT32 coverage through the public `cudnn.pygraph` FROST GEMM engine. Existing direct-JIT INT8 tests do not cover public plan selection and graph execution for these large accumulators.

- Assert a selected `frost_gemm` plan and record public plan names, knobs and workspace in JUnit.
- Compare INT32 output exactly against a CPU INT64 oracle, including odd results above 2^24 that an FP32 roundtrip changes.
- Cover K=2048/4096, positive/negative/minimum INT8 inputs, cancellation, zeros, distinct batches and 144×208 tails.
- Keep one L0 representative with a nondefault stream, poisoned output and three CUDA Graph replays; put the 30-case matrix in L1.
- Reuse the production-derived INT8 MMA architecture gate and restore the caller's stream in `finally`.

This is test coverage: the existing integer precision path passes. There are no production kernel, planner or support-gate changes, and no performance claim. This does not duplicate block-scaled FP4/FP8 replay coverage.

## Validation

Base: `a87ab9d48f81b6a6c42aab997648d28150d850d8`.
Actual Jetson Thor, SM110 (20 SMs); isolated editable checkout verified at runtime. Frontend 1.31.0, cuDNN 9.20, PyTorch 2.13.0+cu130, CUDA toolkit 13.2.78, CuTe DSL 4.7.1. Build-tool deviation: Ninja 1.13.2 rather than the pyproject build pin 1.11.1.1.

From `test/python`:

```bash
python -m pytest --collect-only -q gemm/frost
python -m pytest -o addopts= -m 'L0 or L1' -q   gemm/frost/test_int8_public_contract.py --junitxml=int8-public.xml
```

- Directory-level default L0 collection includes the new representative.
- New matrix: **31 passed, 0 skipped** (6.63 s).
- Existing direct INT8 baseline: **10 passed**; planner baseline: **116 passed**.
- Detector control: temporarily suppress execution in the test only → fails with poisoned `-777` versus expected `33032065`; restore → passes.
- Detector control: temporarily round output through FP32 → fails with `33032064` versus `33032065`; restore → passes.
- Both mutations were restored and are not included in this PR.
- Applicable pre-commit hooks pass (Black and SPDX).

GPU results above cover Thor only. The existing service remained running; timings are test durations, not isolated performance measurements.

## Current develop verification (2026-09-30)

Rebased onto `14a1e999fd50ba3437aa3838c516d6e6854fd768`. A fresh private editable build and verified import path on Thor passed; collection found all 31 cases and the full matrix passed again (31 passed, zero skips). Applicable pre-commit hooks passed. The earlier detector controls remain evidence from the original tested revision. No production performance change is included.

<details>
<summary>🧹 Nitpick comments (1)</summary><blockquote>

<details>
<summary>test/python/gemm/frost/test_int8_public_contract.py (1)</summary><blockquote>

`67-88`: _🎯 Functional Correctness_ | _🔵 Trivial_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Exercise different handle and current streams in the eager check.**

`pygraph.execute` resolves the supplied handle stream and `frost_gemm` launches on it. This test sets that stream equal to PyTorch's current stream, so a regression that launches on the current stream can pass the eager check. Capture and replay also use `stream` as the current stream, so they do not detect it. Add a stream-ordering check that restores poisoned inputs on the handle stream before eager execution while another stream remains current.

<details><summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
         cudnn.set_stream(cudnn_handle, stream.cuda_stream)
-        stream.wait_stream(torch.cuda.current_stream())
-        with torch.cuda.stream(stream):
-            output.fill_(-777)
-            graph.execute(pack, workspace, handle=cudnn_handle)
+        current = torch.cuda.current_stream()
+        saved_a, saved_b = a.clone(), b.clone()
+        a.fill_(127)
+        b.fill_(127)
+        output.fill_(-777)
+        current.synchronize()
+        with torch.cuda.stream(stream):
+            torch.cuda._sleep(1_000_000_000)
+            a.copy_(saved_a)
+            b.copy_(saved_b)
+        graph.execute(pack, workspace, handle=cudnn_handle)
         stream.synchronize()
```

</details>
